### F-35C

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0c/VFA-147_F-35C_lands_on_USS_George_Washington.jpg/500px-VFA-147_F-35C_lands_on_USS_George_Washington.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:VFA-147_F-35C_lands_on_USS_George_Washington.jpg) A [US Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Navy "U.S. Navy") F-35C conducting a landing on the [USS George Washington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_George_Washington_(CVN-73) "USS George Washington (CVN-73)").

The F-35C is the [carrier-based](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carrier-based_aircraft "Carrier-based aircraft") variant of the aircraft, designed for [catapult-assisted take-off and barrier-arrested recovery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CATOBAR "CATOBAR") (CATOBAR) operations from [aircraft carriers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aircraft_carrier "Aircraft carrier"). Compared to the F-35A, the F-35C incorporates several modifications to meet the specific demands of carrier operations. These include larger wings with [foldable wingtips](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folding_wing "Folding wing") to reduce the aircraft's footprint for storage, enlarged control surfaces for improved handling at low speeds, a more robust tailhook for repeated use with carrier [arrestor cables](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arrestor_cable "Arrestor cable"), a twin-wheel nose gear, and reinforced landing gear to withstand the stresses of arrested landings.[&#8202;](#ref-back-newhook-1)[\[264\]](#refnote-newhook) The increased wing area also allows for lower landing speeds and improves both range and payload capacity. The F-35C is limited to a maximum load factor of 7.5g.[&#8202;](#ref-back-FY2019_President's_Budget_Selected_Acquisition_Report_(SAR-0)[\[406\]](#refnote-FY2019_President's_Budget_Selected_Acquisition_Report_(SAR)_%E2%80%93_F-35_Joint_Strike_Fighter_Aircraft_(F-35)-423)

### F-35I "Adir"

Main article: [Lockheed Martin F-35 Lightning II Israeli procurement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_Martin_F-35_Lightning_II_Israeli_procurement "Lockheed Martin F-35 Lightning II Israeli procurement")

The F-35I _Adir_ ( [Hebrew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_language "Hebrew language"): אדיר, meaning "Awesome",[&#8202;](#ref-back-424-0)[\[407\]](#refnote-424) or "Mighty One"[&#8202;](#ref-back-425-0)[\[408\]](#refnote-425)) is an F-35A with unique Israeli modifications. The US initially refused to allow such changes before permitting Israel to integrate its own electronic warfare systems, including sensors and countermeasures. The F-35I _Adir_ is equipped with Israeli-made electronic warfare systems layered over the existing avionics and enhanced data gathering and processing capabilities. It comes with a specialized helmet designed for the aircraft's systems. While many of the details are classified, it is known that the helmet is equipped with a projector for each eye that feeds information directly to the pilots, eliminating the need to look down at cockpit instruments, and tracks the pilot's head movements to know where the pilot is looking as it relays data. The main computer has a plug-and-play function for add-on systems; proposals include an [external jamming pod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radar_jamming_and_deception "Radar jamming and deception"), and new Israeli air-to-air missiles and guided bombs in the internal weapon bays. Due to unique component requirements, the F-35I's wings are locally manufactured.[&#8202;](#ref-back-426-0)[\[409\]](#refnote-426)[&#8202;](#ref-back-427-0)[\[410\]](#refnote-427)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Alon_Ben-David-0)[\[411\]](#refnote-Alon_Ben-David)[&#8202;](#ref-back-AW_Israel_July2011-0)[\[412\]](#refnote-AW_Israel_July2011) A senior IAF official said that the F-35's stealth may be partly overcome within 10 years despite a 30 to 40-year service life, thus Israel's insistence on using its own electronic warfare systems.[&#8202;](#ref-back-430-0)[\[413\]](#refnote-430) In 2010, [Israel Aerospace Industries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Aerospace_Industries "Israel Aerospace Industries") (IAI) considered a two-seat F-35 concept; an IAI executive noted that there was a "known demand for two seats not only from Israel but from other air forces."[&#8202;](#ref-back-431-0)[\[414\]](#refnote-431) In 2008, IAI planned to produce [conformal fuel tanks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conformal_fuel_tank "Conformal fuel tank"), as well as stealthy external fuel tanks;[&#8202;](#ref-back-432-0)[\[415\]](#refnote-432)[&#8202;](#ref-back-433-0)[\[416\]](#refnote-433) these were reportedly used during the [June 2025 Israeli airstrikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/June_2025_Israeli_strikes_on_Iran "June 2025 Israeli strikes on Iran") against Iran.[&#8202;](#ref-back-auto2-1)[\[370\]](#refnote-auto2)

It is widely speculated that the F-35I may have the [capability to operate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_weapons_of_Israel#F-35I "Nuclear weapons of Israel") as a [delivery vehicle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_weapons_delivery "Nuclear weapons delivery") for [Israeli nuclear weapons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_nuclear_weapons "Israeli nuclear weapons"), as it is a derivative of the nuclear-capable F-35A.[&#8202;](#ref-back-434-0)[\[417\]](#refnote-434)[&#8202;](#ref-back-435-0)[\[418\]](#refnote-435)[&#8202;](#ref-back-k450-0)[\[419\]](#refnote-k450) In 2018, Israeli [Channel 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Channel_2_(Israel) "Channel 2 (Israel)") reported in 2014 that, following Lockheed Martin's nuclear-capable upgrades, allowing some F-35As' weapons systems and software to interface with US [permissive action links](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Permissive_action_link "Permissive action link"), a senior US official had declined to comment on whether Israel had requested the same upgrade to its F-35Is.[&#8202;](#ref-back-f408-0)[\[420\]](#refnote-f408)

### Proposed variant

#### CF-35

Main article: [Lockheed Martin F-35 Lightning II Canadian procurement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_Martin_F-35_Lightning_II_Canadian_procurement "Lockheed Martin F-35 Lightning II Canadian procurement")

The Canadian CF-35 was a proposed variant that would differ from the F-35A through the addition of a [drogue parachute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drogue_parachute "Drogue parachute") and the potential inclusion of an F-35B/C-style refueling probe.[&#8202;](#ref-back-auto-1)[\[400\]](#refnote-auto)[&#8202;](#ref-back-C4Wauto-0)[\[421\]](#refnote-C4Wauto) In 2012, it was revealed that the CF-35 would employ the same boom refueling system as the F-35A.[&#8202;](#ref-back-oCanada-2012-0)[\[422\]](#refnote-oCanada-2012-12) One alternative proposal would have been the adoption of the F-35C for its probe refueling and lower landing speed; however, the Parliamentary Budget Officer's report cited the F-35C's limited performance and payload as being too high a price to pay.[&#8202;](#ref-back-C4Wauto-1)[\[423\]](#refnote-C4Wauto) Following the [2015 Federal Election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Canadian_federal_election "2015 Canadian federal election"), the [Liberal Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberal_Party_of_Canada "Liberal Party of Canada"), whose campaign had included a pledge to cancel the F-35 procurement,[&#8202;](#ref-back-Liberal2015Platform-0)[\[424\]](#refnote-Liberal2015Platform) formed a new government and commenced an open competition to replace the existing [CF-18 Hornet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McDonnell_Douglas_CF-18_Hornet "McDonnell Douglas CF-18 Hornet").[&#8202;](#ref-back-canada_exit-0)[\[425\]](#refnote-canada_exit) The CF-35 variant was deemed too expensive to develop, and was never considered. The Canadian government decided not to pursue any other modifications in the [Future Fighter Capability Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Future_Fighter_Capability_Project "Future Fighter Capability Project"), and instead focused on the potential procurement of the existing F-35A variant.[&#8202;](#ref-back-443-0)[\[426\]](#refnote-443)

On 28 March 2022, the Canadian Government began negotiations with Lockheed Martin for 88 F-35As[&#8202;](#ref-back-Brewster_28Mar22-0)[\[427\]](#refnote-Brewster_28Mar22) to replace the aging fleet of CF-18 fighters starting in 2025.[&#8202;](#ref-back-445-0)[\[428\]](#refnote-445) The aircraft are reported to cost up to CA$19bn total with a life-cycle cost estimated at CA$77bn over the course of the F-35 program.[&#8202;](#ref-back-446-0)[\[429\]](#refnote-446)[&#8202;](#ref-back-447-0)[\[430\]](#refnote-447) On 9 January 2023, Canada formally confirmed the purchase of 88 aircraft. The initial delivery to the [Royal Canadian Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Canadian_Air_Force "Royal Canadian Air Force") in 2026 will be 4 aircraft, followed by 6 aircraft each in 2027–2028, and the rest to be delivered by 2032.[&#8202;](#ref-back-448-0)[\[431\]](#refnote-448)[&#8202;](#ref-back-449-0)[\[432\]](#refnote-449) The additional characteristics confirmed for Canadian F-35As included the drag chute pod for landings at short/icy arctic runways, as well as the 'sidekick' system, which allows the CF-35 to carry up to 6 x [AIM-120D](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AIM-120D "AIM-120D") missiles internally (instead of the typical internal capacity of 4 x AIM-120 missiles on other variants).[&#8202;](#ref-back-450-0)[\[433\]](#refnote-450)

### New export variant

In December 2021, it was reported that Lockheed Martin was developing a new variant for an unspecified foreign customer. The Department of Defense released US$49million in funding for this work.[&#8202;](#ref-back-451-0)[\[434\]](#refnote-451)

- [Belgian Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belgian_Air_Force "Belgian Air Force") – 12 delivered, of which 8 trainer aircraft are based at [Luke Air Force Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luke_Air_Force_Base "Luke Air Force Base") in the United States.[&#8202;](#ref-back-452-0)[\[435\]](#refnote-452)[&#8202;](#ref-back-453-0)[\[436\]](#refnote-453) 34 F-35As planned in 2018 as of 2019[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lockheed_Martin_F-35_Lightning_II&action=edit),[&#8202;](#ref-back-454-0)[\[437\]](#refnote-454)[&#8202;](#ref-back-455-0)[\[438\]](#refnote-455) order for 11 additional F-35As pending.[&#8202;](#ref-back-456-0)[\[439\]](#refnote-456)

- [Royal Canadian Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Canadian_Air_Force "Royal Canadian Air Force") – 88 F-35As (Block 4) ordered on 9 January 2023. Canada has paid for 30 aircraft to be delivered between 2026 and 2029.[&#8202;](#ref-back-457-0)[\[440\]](#refnote-457) The plan was for the remainder to be delivered by 2032, but Canada decided in early 2025 to review the remaining 58 aircraft, possibly replacing them with [Saab Gripen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saab_Gripen "Saab Gripen"). A preliminary audit indicated that the program's cost had escalated to $27.7billion from an initial $19billion.[&#8202;](#ref-back-458-0)[\[441\]](#refnote-458)[&#8202;](#ref-back-459-0)[\[442\]](#refnote-459) The first eight aircraft will be based at [Luke Air Force Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luke_Air_Force_Base "Luke Air Force Base") for pilot training.[&#8202;](#ref-back-460-0)[\[443\]](#refnote-460) The aircraft are to replace [CF-18s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McDonnell_Douglas_CF-18_Hornet "McDonnell Douglas CF-18 Hornet") delivered in the 1980s.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Berthiaume-0)[\[444\]](#refnote-Berthiaume)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Brewster090123-0)[\[445\]](#refnote-Brewster090123) Canada's first two CF-35As were delivered in October 2026.[&#8202;](#ref-back-463-0)[\[446\]](#refnote-463)

The next patch of eight F-35As are to be delivered to Finland[&#8202;](#ref-back-473-0)[\[456\]](#refnote-473)[&#8202;](#ref-back-474-0)[\[457\]](#refnote-474)[&#8202;](#ref-back-475-0)[\[458\]](#refnote-475) by the end of September 2026.[&#8202;](#ref-back-476-0)[\[459\]](#refnote-476) The first Finnish F-35As are delivered in TR-3 configuration.[&#8202;](#ref-back-477-0)[\[460\]](#refnote-477)

![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/b/ba/Flag_of_Germany.svg/60px-Flag_of_Germany.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)[Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Germany "Germany")

- [German Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_Air_Force "German Air Force") – In 2022, ordered 35 F-35As[&#8202;](#ref-back-478-0)[\[461\]](#refnote-478)[&#8202;](#ref-back-479-0)[\[462\]](#refnote-479) for delivery starting in 2026.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:9-0)[\[463\]](#refnote-:9)As of 2024,[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lockheed_Martin_F-35_Lightning_II&action=edit) an order for an additional 10 was being considered.[&#8202;](#ref-back-481-0)[\[464\]](#refnote-481) German F-35s will also replace the older [Panavia Tornados](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panavia_Tornado "Panavia Tornado") in carrying the [B61 nuclear bomb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B61_nuclear_bomb "B61 nuclear bomb").[&#8202;](#ref-back-:9-1)[\[463\]](#refnote-:9) Its first jet was delivered on 18 September 2026.[&#8202;](#ref-back-482-0)[\[465\]](#refnote-482)

![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d4/Flag_of_Israel.svg/60px-Flag_of_Israel.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)[Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel "Israel")

- [Israeli Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Air_Force "Israeli Air Force") – 48 F-35I "Adir"s delivered as of January 2026.[&#8202;](#ref-back-483-0)[\[466\]](#refnote-483)[&#8202;](#ref-back-484-0)[\[467\]](#refnote-484) Includes one F-35 [testbed aircraft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Testbed_aircraft "Testbed aircraft") for indigenous Israeli weapons, electronics, and structural upgrades, designated AS-15.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Israel_F-35i-0)[\[468\]](#refnote-Israel_F-35i)[&#8202;](#ref-back-jpost.com-0)[\[469\]](#refnote-jpost.com) A total of 100 ordered.[&#8202;](#ref-back-487-0)[\[470\]](#refnote-487)

![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/0/03/Flag_of_Italy.svg/60px-Flag_of_Italy.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)[Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italy "Italy")

- [Italian Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Air_Force "Italian Air Force") – 24 F-35As and 8 F-35Bs delivered as of April2025[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lockheed_Martin_F-35_Lightning_II&action=edit),[&#8202;](#ref-back-488-0)[\[471\]](#refnote-488) of 75 F-35As and 20 F-35Bs ordered for the Italian Air Force.[&#8202;](#ref-back-ItalyPlans-0)[\[472\]](#refnote-ItalyPlans)[&#8202;](#ref-back-LockheedItaly-0)[\[473\]](#refnote-LockheedItaly)[&#8202;](#ref-back-italy-0)[\[474\]](#refnote-italy)[&#8202;](#ref-back-492-0)[\[475\]](#refnote-492)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Giansiracusa-0)[\[476\]](#refnote-Giansiracusa)
- [Italian Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Navy "Italian Navy") – 6 delivered as of September2024[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lockheed_Martin_F-35_Lightning_II&action=edit), of 20 F-35Bs ordered for the Italian Navy.[&#8202;](#ref-back-ItalyPlans-1)[\[472\]](#refnote-ItalyPlans)[&#8202;](#ref-back-LockheedItaly-1)[\[473\]](#refnote-LockheedItaly)[&#8202;](#ref-back-italy-1)[\[474\]](#refnote-italy)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Giansiracusa-1)[\[476\]](#refnote-Giansiracusa)[&#8202;](#ref-back-:8-1)[\[381\]](#refnote-:8)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6f/F-35A_from_the_Japan_Air_Force.jpg/500px-F-35A_from_the_Japan_Air_Force.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:F-35A_from_the_Japan_Air_Force.jpg) A Japan Air Self-Defense Force F-35A in flight![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/9e/Flag_of_Japan.svg/60px-Flag_of_Japan.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)[Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japan "Japan")

- [Japan Air Self-Defense Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japan_Air_Self-Defense_Force "Japan Air Self-Defense Force") – 47 F-35As and F-35Bs delivered and operational as of December 2025[&#8202;](#ref-back-494-0)[\[477\]](#refnote-494)[&#8202;](#ref-back-495-0)[\[478\]](#refnote-495) with a total order of 147, including 105 F-35As and 42 F-35Bs.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Diplomat-0)[\[479\]](#refnote-Diplomat)[&#8202;](#ref-back-497-0)[\[480\]](#refnote-497)[&#8202;](#ref-back-498-0)[\[481\]](#refnote-498)[&#8202;](#ref-back-doj2022-0)[\[482\]](#refnote-doj2022)

![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/20/Flag_of_the_Netherlands.svg/60px-Flag_of_the_Netherlands.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)[Netherlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netherlands "Netherlands")

- [Royal Netherlands Air and Space Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Netherlands_Air_and_Space_Force "Royal Netherlands Air and Space Force") – 48 F-35As delivered and operational, of which 8 trainer aircraft are based at [Luke Air Force Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luke_Air_Force_Base "Luke Air Force Base") in the US.[&#8202;](#ref-back-auto3-1)[\[396\]](#refnote-auto3) 58 F-35As ordered in total.[&#8202;](#ref-back-World_Air_Forces_2014-0)[\[483\]](#refnote-World_Air_Forces_2014)[&#8202;](#ref-back-RNAF-0)[\[484\]](#refnote-RNAF)[&#8202;](#ref-back-502-0)[\[485\]](#refnote-502) The RNLASF is the second air force with a 5th gen-only fighter fleet after the retirement of its F-16s.[&#8202;](#ref-back-503-0)[\[486\]](#refnote-503)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/62/Norwegian_F-35_is_refueled_during_CW24_2_%288676159%29.jpg/500px-Norwegian_F-35_is_refueled_during_CW24_2_%288676159%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Norwegian_F-35_is_refueled_during_CW24_2_(8676159).jpg) An F-35A Lightning II of the [Royal Norwegian Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Norwegian_Air_Force "Royal Norwegian Air Force") refueled by a KC-135 Stratotanker over the UK in September 2024![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d9/Flag_of_Norway.svg/60px-Flag_of_Norway.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)[Norway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norway "Norway")

- [Royal Norwegian Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Norwegian_Air_Force "Royal Norwegian Air Force") – 52 F-35As delivered.[&#8202;](#ref-back-504-0)[\[487\]](#refnote-504) They differ from other F-35As through the addition of a [drogue parachute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drogue_parachute "Drogue parachute").[&#8202;](#ref-back-505-0)[\[488\]](#refnote-505)

![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/1/12/Flag_of_Poland.svg/60px-Flag_of_Poland.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)[Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poland "Poland")

- [Polish Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_Air_Force "Polish Air Force") – 32 F-35A "Husarz" Block 4 jets with "Technology Refresh 3" software update and drogue parachutes ordered, with 8 delivered as of 2025.[&#8202;](#ref-back-506-0)[\[489\]](#refnote-506)[&#8202;](#ref-back-507-0)[\[490\]](#refnote-507)[&#8202;](#ref-back-508-0)[\[491\]](#refnote-508)[&#8202;](#ref-back-509-0)[\[492\]](#refnote-509)[&#8202;](#ref-back-pl_order-0)[\[493\]](#refnote-pl_order)[&#8202;](#ref-back-511-0)[\[494\]](#refnote-511)[&#8202;](#ref-back-:7-0)[\[495\]](#refnote-:7) There are plans to order an additional 32 F-35s, which will comprise two squadrons.[&#8202;](#ref-back-513-0)[\[496\]](#refnote-513) The first domestic flights of the F-35 by Polish pilots took place in February 2025, signaling the start of the country's use of the aircraft.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:7-1)[\[495\]](#refnote-:7)

![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/09/Flag_of_South_Korea.svg/60px-Flag_of_South_Korea.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)[South Korea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Korea "South Korea")

- [Republic of Korea Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Korea_Air_Force "Republic of Korea Air Force") – 40 F-35As ordered and delivered as of January2022[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lockheed_Martin_F-35_Lightning_II&action=edit),[&#8202;](#ref-back-514-0)[\[497\]](#refnote-514) with 25 more ordered in September 2023.[&#8202;](#ref-back-515-0)[\[498\]](#refnote-515)[&#8202;](#ref-back-516-0)[\[499\]](#refnote-516)[&#8202;](#ref-back-517-0)[\[500\]](#refnote-517)[&#8202;](#ref-back-518-0)[\[501\]](#refnote-518)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/05/Britain%27s_most_advanced_jets_touch_down_on_home_soil_MOD_45164370.jpg/500px-Britain%27s_most_advanced_jets_touch_down_on_home_soil_MOD_45164370.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Britain's_most_advanced_jets_touch_down_on_home_soil_MOD_45164370.jpg) First four RAF F-35Bs on a delivery flight to [RAF Marham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RAF_Marham "RAF Marham"), June 2018![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a5/Flag_of_the_United_Kingdom_%281-2%29.svg/60px-Flag_of_the_United_Kingdom_%281-2%29.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)[United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Kingdom "United Kingdom")

- [Royal Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Air_Force "Royal Air Force") and [Royal Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Navy "Royal Navy") (owned by the RAF but jointly operated) – 48 F-35Bs received, of which 47 are operational after the loss of one aircraft in November 2021.[&#8202;](#ref-back-519-0)[\[502\]](#refnote-519)[&#8202;](#ref-back-520-0)[\[503\]](#refnote-520)[&#8202;](#ref-back-521-0)[\[504\]](#refnote-521)[&#8202;](#ref-back-auto4-1)[\[338\]](#refnote-auto4)[&#8202;](#ref-back-auto1-0)[\[505\]](#refnote-auto1)[&#8202;](#ref-back-523-0)[\[506\]](#refnote-523)[&#8202;](#ref-back-itv.com-0)[\[507\]](#refnote-itv.com) As of 2018, three of the UK's aircraft were in the US being used for testing and training.[&#8202;](#ref-back-marham-0)[\[508\]](#refnote-marham) The first order of 48 was completed in 2026. A total of 138 are planned to be procured during the lifetime of the program.[&#8202;](#ref-back-526-0)[\[509\]](#refnote-526) While the UK was originally expected to only order the F-35B, in June 2025 the UK announced plans to procure 12 F-35As, enabling the UK to join [NATO's nuclear mission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_sharing "Nuclear sharing") due to the F-35A's ability to carry B61 nuclear bombs internally, alongside 15 F-35Bs.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:10-1)[\[342\]](#refnote-:10)[&#8202;](#ref-back-:11-1)[\[344\]](#refnote-:11) With the completion of the initial order of 48 aircraft, a secondary order of 27 aircraft (12 F-35As and 15 F-35Bs) is expected to be procured by 2033.[&#8202;](#ref-back-527-0)[\[510\]](#refnote-527)[&#8202;](#ref-back-528-0)[\[511\]](#refnote-528)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/ba/F-35C_Lightning_II_aircraft_are_tested_aboard_USS_Abraham_Lincoln._%2836272619443%29.jpg/500px-F-35C_Lightning_II_aircraft_are_tested_aboard_USS_Abraham_Lincoln._%2836272619443%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:F-35C_Lightning_II_aircraft_are_tested_aboard_USS_Abraham_Lincoln._(36272619443).jpg) USN F-35C performs a [touch-and-go landing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Touch-and-go_landing "Touch-and-go landing") aboard [USS _Abraham Lincoln_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Abraham_Lincoln_(CVN-72) "USS Abraham Lincoln (CVN-72)").![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/5/5d/Flag_of_the_United_States_%2823px%29.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail_unscaled)[United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States "United States")

At least 850 were ordered in 2026 for the branches of the [United States Armed Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Armed_Forces "United States Armed Forces"), to be delivered over the next 10 years.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Reuters2026_850_F-0)[\[512\]](#refnote-Reuters2026_850_F)

- [United States Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Air_Force "United States Air Force") – 400+ delivered, with 1,763 F-35As planned.[&#8202;](#ref-back-530-0)[\[513\]](#refnote-530)
- [United States Marine Corps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Marine_Corps "United States Marine Corps") – 112 F-35B/Cs delivered,[&#8202;](#ref-back-theaviationist_2024-0)[\[514\]](#refnote-theaviationist_2024) with 280 F-35Bs and 140 F-35Cs planned.[&#8202;](#ref-back-532-0)[\[515\]](#refnote-532)
- [United States Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Navy "United States Navy") – 110+ delivered,[&#8202;](#ref-back-theaviationist_2024-1)[\[514\]](#refnote-theaviationist_2024) with 273 F-35Cs planned.[&#8202;](#ref-back-myp-0)[\[516\]](#refnote-myp)

### Future operators

![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cb/Flag_of_the_Czech_Republic.svg/60px-Flag_of_the_Czech_Republic.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)[Czech Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Czech_Republic "Czech Republic")

- [Czech Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Czech_Air_Force "Czech Air Force") – On 29 June 2023, the US State Department announced the approval of a possible sale to the Czech Republic of F-35 aircraft, munitions and related equipment worth up to $5.62billion.[&#8202;](#ref-back-534-0)[\[517\]](#refnote-534) On 29 January 2024, the Czech government signed a memorandum of understanding with the US to buy 24 F-35As.[&#8202;](#ref-back-535-0)[\[518\]](#refnote-535) In September 2024, the Czech Republic signed a contract for F-35A logistics support.[&#8202;](#ref-back-536-0)[\[519\]](#refnote-536)

![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5c/Flag_of_Greece.svg/60px-Flag_of_Greece.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)[Greece](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greece "Greece")

- [Hellenic Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hellenic_Air_Force "Hellenic Air Force") – In 2024, Greece ordered 20 F-35As for delivery in late 2027 to early 2028,[&#8202;](#ref-back-537-0)[\[520\]](#refnote-537)[&#8202;](#ref-back-538-0)[\[521\]](#refnote-538) with an option to buy 20 more.[&#8202;](#ref-back-539-0)[\[522\]](#refnote-539) The Greek F-35A production for 20 aircraft will "begin" with Lot 20, with full production starting in 2027, first delivery at the end of 2028, while the arrival of the first aircraft in Greece is expected for 2030, as per the [VP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/VP "VP") _for_ _Strategy and Business Development_ and the VP _for Customer Requirements_ of [Lockheed Martin Aeronautics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_Martin_Aeronautics "Lockheed Martin Aeronautics").[&#8202;](#ref-back-540-0)[\[523\]](#refnote-540)[&#8202;](#ref-back-541-0)[\[524\]](#refnote-541) The date has changed for November 2029 as [United States Secretary of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Secretary_of_State "United States Secretary of State") [Marco Rubio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marco_Rubio "Marco Rubio") will meet up with [Kyriakos Mitsotakis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyriakos_Mitsotakis "Kyriakos Mitsotakis") in the 7 September 2026 to discuss so speed up the processe for Greece to receive the F-35's.[&#8202;](#ref-back-542-0)[\[525\]](#refnote-542)

![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/73/Flag_of_Romania.svg/60px-Flag_of_Romania.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)[Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romania "Romania")

- [Romanian Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanian_Air_Force "Romanian Air Force") – Romania signed a contract for 32 F-35As worth $6.5billion on 21 November 2024,[&#8202;](#ref-back-543-0)[\[526\]](#refnote-543) planning to buy 48 F-35As in two phases – a first phase of 32 and a second phase of 16. The first F-35As will arrive after 2030 and will replace the current Romanian F-16 fleet between 2034 and 2040.[&#8202;](#ref-back-544-0)[\[527\]](#refnote-544)[&#8202;](#ref-back-545-0)[\[528\]](#refnote-545)

![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/48/Flag_of_Singapore.svg/60px-Flag_of_Singapore.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)[Singapore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Singapore "Singapore")

- [Republic of Singapore Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Singapore_Air_Force "Republic of Singapore Air Force") – 8 F-35As and 12 F-35Bs on order as of February2024[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lockheed_Martin_F-35_Lightning_II&action=edit). The first 4 F-35Bs are to be delivered in 2026, while the other 8 are to be delivered in 2028. The 8 F-35As are expected to arrive by 2030.[&#8202;](#ref-back-546-0)[\[529\]](#refnote-546)[&#8202;](#ref-back-547-0)[\[530\]](#refnote-547)

![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/08/Flag_of_Switzerland_%28Pantone%29.svg/40px-Flag_of_Switzerland_%28Pantone%29.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)[Switzerland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Switzerland "Switzerland")

- [Swiss Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swiss_Air_Force "Swiss Air Force") – 36 F-35As ordered to replace the current [F-5E/F Tiger II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northrop_F-5 "Northrop F-5") and [F/A-18C/D Hornet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McDonnell_Douglas_F/A-18_Hornet "McDonnell Douglas F/A-18 Hornet"). Deliveries will begin in 2027 and conclude in 2030.[&#8202;](#ref-back-548-0)[\[531\]](#refnote-548)[&#8202;](#ref-back-549-0)[\[532\]](#refnote-549) In September 2025, it was announced that Lockheed failed to meet the contract price of $7.55B, and that it would be increased by $1.63B.[&#8202;](#ref-back-550-0)[\[533\]](#refnote-550) As a result, the Swiss decided to reduce the order to a lower number of planes in December 2025.[&#8202;](#ref-back-551-0)[\[534\]](#refnote-551) In January 2026, it was reported that the support infrastructure was also behind schedule and over budget, according to Switzerland's federal auditors.[&#8202;](#ref-back-552-0)[\[535\]](#refnote-552)

### Potential operators

![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2c/Flag_of_Morocco.svg/60px-Flag_of_Morocco.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)[Morocco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morocco "Morocco")

- [Royal Moroccan Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Moroccan_Air_Force "Royal Moroccan Air Force") – As of October2025[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lockheed_Martin_F-35_Lightning_II&action=edit), Morocco has entered into negotiations with the United States for the acquisition of the F-35 Lightning II stealth multirole fighter for its air force. These discussions have been ongoing for several months, with documents indicating that Israel has granted its approval in principle for the sale. This authorization is provided within the framework of the [Qualitative Military Edge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qualitative_Military_Edge "Qualitative Military Edge"), a clause of the Arms Export Control Act requiring that no arms sale in the region compromise Israel's military superiority. If finalized, the agreement would make Morocco the first African and Arab nation to operate F-35s.[&#8202;](#ref-back-553-0)[\[536\]](#refnote-553)

![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a8/Flag_of_Portugal_%28official%29.svg/60px-Flag_of_Portugal_%28official%29.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)[Portugal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portugal "Portugal")

- [Portugal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portugal "Portugal") – The F-35 has been offered to Portugal to replace its ageing fleet of 28 F16 fighters. Other possible candidates include: [Eurofighter Typhoon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurofighter_Typhoon "Eurofighter Typhoon"), [Gripen E](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saab_JAS_39_Gripen "Saab JAS 39 Gripen"), and [Dassault Rafale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dassault_Rafale "Dassault Rafale").[&#8202;](#ref-back-554-0)[\[537\]](#refnote-554)[&#8202;](#ref-back-555-0)[\[538\]](#refnote-555)

![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0d/Flag_of_Saudi_Arabia.svg/60px-Flag_of_Saudi_Arabia.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)[Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Arabia "Saudi Arabia")

- [Royal Saudi Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Saudi_Air_Force "Royal Saudi Air Force") – It was announced in November 2025 that Saudi Arabia would procure 48 F-35As. It is likely that the F-35As operated by the RSAF will lack some more advanced features found on Israel's fleet, to maintain the Qualitative Military Edge, with some American missiles not to be offered to Saudi Arabia.[&#8202;](#ref-back-556-0)[\[539\]](#refnote-556)[&#8202;](#ref-back-557-0)[\[540\]](#refnote-557)[&#8202;](#ref-back-558-0)[\[541\]](#refnote-558) On 17 September 2026, the US Department of State announced that it had approved a potential F-35 sale to Saudi Arabia.[&#8202;](#ref-back-559-0)[\[542\]](#refnote-559)

### Cancelled operators

![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/72/Flag_of_the_Republic_of_China.svg/60px-Flag_of_the_Republic_of_China.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)[Republic of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwan "Taiwan")

- [Republic of China Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_China_Air_Force "Republic of China Air Force") – Taiwan has repeatedly expressed interest in buying the F-35 to deter and fight off any Chinese attempt to seize the island by force. It is reportedly most interested in the F-35B STOVL variant, which could enable the Republic of China Air Force to continue operations if China bombed the island's runways.[&#8202;](#ref-back-560-0)[\[543\]](#refnote-560) But the US has repeatedly rebuffed this interest[&#8202;](#ref-back-561-0)[\[544\]](#refnote-561) – for example, in March 2009, September 2011,[&#8202;](#ref-back-562-0)[\[545\]](#refnote-562) early 2017[&#8202;](#ref-back-563-0)[\[546\]](#refnote-563) and March 2018. The usual reason given is to prevent provoking Beijing. But in April 2018, another reason for US reluctance surfaced: concern that Chinese spies within the [Taiwanese Armed Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_China_Armed_Forces "Republic of China Armed Forces") might gain classified data about the aircraft. In November 2018, it was reported that Taiwanese military leaders had abandoned efforts to buy the F-35 and would instead buy [Rafale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dassault_Rafale "Dassault Rafale")[&#8202;](#ref-back-564-0)[\[547\]](#refnote-564) or a larger number of [F-16V Viper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_Martin_F-16V_Viper "Lockheed Martin F-16V Viper") aircraft. The decision was reportedly motivated by concerns about industry independence, cost, and espionage.[&#8202;](#ref-back-565-0)[\[548\]](#refnote-565)

![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fe/Flag_of_Egypt.svg/60px-Flag_of_Egypt.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)[Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egypt "Egypt")

- [Egyptian Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egyptian_Air_Force "Egyptian Air Force") – Despite US President [Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump "Donald Trump")'s announcement to sell 20 F-35As to Egypt in 2018, strong objections from the US Department of Defense and [Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel "Israel") led to the deal's cancellation.[&#8202;](#ref-back-566-0)[\[549\]](#refnote-566)

![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/4/41/Flag_of_India.svg/60px-Flag_of_India.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)[India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India "India")

- [Indian Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Air_Force "Indian Air Force") – In February 2025, Trump offered the F-35 to [Prime Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_India "Prime Minister of India") [Narendra Modi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Narendra_Modi "Narendra Modi") of India,[&#8202;](#ref-back-567-0)[\[550\]](#refnote-567) which as of March 2025, was also considering a competing offer from Russia's [Sukhoi Su-57](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sukhoi_Su-57 "Sukhoi Su-57").[&#8202;](#ref-back-568-0)[\[551\]](#refnote-568) However, in late July 2025, _[Bloomberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bloomberg_News "Bloomberg News")_ reported that India has already rejected its requirement of the aircraft. While the [Indian Ministry of Defence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Defence_(India) "Ministry of Defence (India)") has not confirmed any of this, the [Indian Ministry of External Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Ministry_of_External_Affairs "Indian Ministry of External Affairs") maintained that India has not held any formal discussions about the aircraft's procurement with the US.[&#8202;](#ref-back-569-0)[\[552\]](#refnote-569)[&#8202;](#ref-back-570-0)[\[553\]](#refnote-570)

![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/9a/Flag_of_Spain.svg/60px-Flag_of_Spain.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)[Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spain "Spain")

- [Spanish Air and Space Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_Air_and_Space_Force "Spanish Air and Space Force") – The Spanish government explored the acquisition of the F-35 through requests for information and preliminary analysis,[&#8202;](#ref-back-571-0)[\[554\]](#refnote-571) but in August 2025 the option was formally ruled out.[&#8202;](#ref-back-572-0)[\[555\]](#refnote-572)

![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a9/Flag_of_Thailand.svg/60px-Flag_of_Thailand.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)[Thailand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thailand "Thailand")

- [Royal Thai Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Thai_Air_Force "Royal Thai Air Force") – 8 or 12 F-35s are planned to replace the [F-16A/B Block 15 ADFs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Dynamics_F-16_Fighting_Falcon_variants#F-16A/B_Block_15_ADF "General Dynamics F-16 Fighting Falcon variants") in service. On 12 January 2022, Thailand's cabinet approved a budget for the first four F-35As, estimated at 13.8billion baht in FY2023.[&#8202;](#ref-back-573-0)[\[556\]](#refnote-573)[&#8202;](#ref-back-574-0)[\[557\]](#refnote-574)[&#8202;](#ref-back-575-0)[\[558\]](#refnote-575) On 22 May 2023, the [United States Department of Defense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Defense "United States Department of Defense") implied it will turn down Thailand's bid to buy F-35s, and instead offer [F-16 Block 70/72 Viper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Dynamics_F-16_Fighting_Falcon_variants#F-16_Block_70/72 "General Dynamics F-16 Fighting Falcon variants") and [F-15EX Eagle II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boeing_F-15EX_Eagle_II "Boeing F-15EX Eagle II") fighters, a Royal Thai Air Force source said.[&#8202;](#ref-back-576-0)[\[559\]](#refnote-576)

![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b4/Flag_of_Turkey.svg/60px-Flag_of_Turkey.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)[Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkey "Turkey")

- On 11 July 2002, Turkey became a Level 3 partner of the [F-35 Joint Strike Fighter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F-35_Joint_Strike_Fighter "F-35 Joint Strike Fighter") (JSF) development program, and on 25 January 2007, Turkey officially joined the production phase of the JSF program, agreeing to purchase a total of 116 [F-35 Lightning II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F-35_Lightning_II "F-35 Lightning II") aircraft (100 [F-35A](#F-35A) [CTOLs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CTOL "CTOL") for the [Turkish Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Air_Force "Turkish Air Force") and 16 [F-35B](#F-35B) [STOVLs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/STOVL "STOVL") for the [TCG Anadolu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TCG_Anadolu "TCG Anadolu")).[&#8202;](#ref-back-577-0)[\[560\]](#refnote-577) Future purchases have been banned by the US following Turkey's decision to buy the [S-400 missile system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S-400_missile_system "S-400 missile system") from Russia, with contracts canceled by early 2020.[&#8202;](#ref-back-turkeyban-0)[\[561\]](#refnote-turkeyban) Six of Turkey's 30 ordered F-35As were completed as of 2019[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lockheed_Martin_F-35_Lightning_II&action=edit) (they are still kept in a hangar in the United States as of 2023[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lockheed_Martin_F-35_Lightning_II&action=edit)[&#8202;](#ref-back-FlightGlobal-23.01.2023-0)[\[562\]](#refnote-FlightGlobal-23.01.2023)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Janes-24.01.2023-0)[\[563\]](#refnote-Janes-24.01.2023) and so far haven't been transferred to the USAF, despite a modification in the 2020 fiscal year defense budget by the US Congress which gives authority to do so if necessary),[&#8202;](#ref-back-TRT_World-21.07.2020-0)[\[564\]](#refnote-TRT_World-21.07.2020)[&#8202;](#ref-back-DefenseNews-20.07.2020-0)[\[565\]](#refnote-DefenseNews-20.07.2020) and two more were at the assembly line in 2020.[&#8202;](#ref-back-TRT_World-21.07.2020-1)[\[564\]](#refnote-TRT_World-21.07.2020)[&#8202;](#ref-back-DefenseNews-20.07.2020-1)[\[565\]](#refnote-DefenseNews-20.07.2020) The first four F-35As were delivered to [Luke Air Force Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luke_Air_Force_Base "Luke Air Force Base") in 2018[&#8202;](#ref-back-583-0)[\[566\]](#refnote-583) and 2019[&#8202;](#ref-back-584-0)[\[567\]](#refnote-584) for the training of Turkish pilots.[&#8202;](#ref-back-585-0)[\[568\]](#refnote-585)[&#8202;](#ref-back-586-0)[\[569\]](#refnote-586) On 20 July 2020, the US government had formally approved the seizure of eight F-35As originally bound for Turkey and their transfer to the USAF, together with a contract to modify them to USAF specifications.[&#8202;](#ref-back-587-0)[\[570\]](#refnote-587) The US has not refunded the $1.4billion payment made by Turkey for purchasing the F-35As as of January2023[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lockheed_Martin_F-35_Lightning_II&action=edit).[&#8202;](#ref-back-FlightGlobal-23.01.2023-1)[\[562\]](#refnote-FlightGlobal-23.01.2023)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Janes-24.01.2023-1)[\[563\]](#refnote-Janes-24.01.2023) On 1 February 2024, the United States expressed willingness to readmit Turkey into the F-35 program if Turkey agrees to give up its S-400 system.[&#8202;](#ref-back-588-0)[\[571\]](#refnote-588) After US President Trump and Turkish President Erdoğan's phone call in March 2025, news was reported in the press that Trump could approve the sale of F-35s to Turkey if Turkey resolves the S-400 issue.[&#8202;](#ref-back-589-0)[\[572\]](#refnote-589) Following a meeting between Trump and Erdoğan at the White House on September 25, 2025, it was revealed that one of the proposed solutions, reported by the media in October 2025, stipulates the relocation of Turkey's S-400 missile systems to the [Nakhchivan Autonomous Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nakhchivan_Autonomous_Republic "Nakhchivan Autonomous Republic"), an exclave of [Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijan "Azerbaijan") which has a five-mile (8.0km) border with Turkey, as a precondition for lifting the [Countering America's Adversaries Through Sanctions Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Countering_America's_Adversaries_Through_Sanctions_Act#Turkey "Countering America's Adversaries Through Sanctions Act") (CAATSA) sanctions and approving the sale and transfer of F-35s and [Patriot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MIM-104_Patriot "MIM-104 Patriot") surface-to-air missile systems to Turkey.[&#8202;](#ref-back-590-0)[\[573\]](#refnote-590) On 24 June 2026, during a joint press conference with NATO Secretary General [Mark Rutte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Rutte "Mark Rutte") at the White House, Trump stated that he would "probably do something that will make Turkey very happy" regarding Turkey's request to rejoin the F-35 program, ahead of the [NATO Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Ankara_NATO_summit "2026 Ankara NATO summit") in Ankara.[&#8202;](#ref-back-591-0)[\[574\]](#refnote-591) The same day, Reuters reported that the US administration was expected to approve the export of [General Electric F110](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Electric_F110 "General Electric F110") engines for Turkey's [KAAN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TAI_TF_Kaan "TAI TF Kaan") fighter program before the summit, despite opposition in US Congress.[&#8202;](#ref-back-592-0)[\[575\]](#refnote-592)[&#8202;](#ref-back-593-0)[\[576\]](#refnote-593) Turkey's Ministry of National Defense announced that diplomatic consultations with US counterparts on the country's potential return to the F-35 program and the removal of related restrictions were continuing. US officials, however, emphasized that any policy shift would depend on addressing concerns regarding Turkey's possession of the Russian [S-400](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S-400 "S-400") air defense system.[&#8202;](#ref-back-594-0)[\[577\]](#refnote-594)[&#8202;](#ref-back-595-0)[\[578\]](#refnote-595)[&#8202;](#ref-back-596-0)[\[579\]](#refnote-596)

![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cb/Flag_of_the_United_Arab_Emirates.svg/60px-Flag_of_the_United_Arab_Emirates.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)[United Arab Emirates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Arab_Emirates "United Arab Emirates")

- [United Arab Emirates Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Arab_Emirates_Air_Force "United Arab Emirates Air Force") – Up to 50 F-35As planned.[&#8202;](#ref-back-597-0)[\[580\]](#refnote-597) On 27 January 2021, the Biden administration temporarily suspended the F-35 sales to the UAE.[&#8202;](#ref-back-598-0)[\[581\]](#refnote-598) After pausing the bill to review the sale, the Biden administration confirmed it would move forward with the deal on 13 April 2021.[&#8202;](#ref-back-599-0)[\[582\]](#refnote-599) In December 2021, the UAE withdrew from purchasing F-35s as they did not agree to the additional terms of the transaction from the US.[&#8202;](#ref-back-600-0)[\[583\]](#refnote-600)[&#8202;](#ref-back-601-0)[\[584\]](#refnote-601) On 14 September 2024, a senior UAE official said that the UAE does not expect to resume talks with the US about the F-35.[&#8202;](#ref-back-602-0)[\[585\]](#refnote-602)

## Accidents and notable incidents

Main article: [List of accidents and incidents involving the Lockheed Martin F-35 Lightning II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_accidents_and_incidents_involving_the_Lockheed_Martin_F-35_Lightning_II "List of accidents and incidents involving the Lockheed Martin F-35 Lightning II")

The F-35 is a statistically safe military aircraft, described as being twice as safe as the F-16.[&#8202;](#ref-back-603-0)[\[586\]](#refnote-603) Still, since 2014, more than a dozen have crashed or otherwise been involved in incidents that have killed or severely injured people or destroyed the aircraft. Some were caused by operator error; others by mechanical problems, some of which set the entire program back.[&#8202;](#ref-back-604-0)[\[587\]](#refnote-604)

As of August2026[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lockheed_Martin_F-35_Lightning_II&action=edit), there were 12 crashes causing the loss of an F-35 airframe, primarily in the US.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:20-1)[\[390\]](#refnote-:20)

The only known fatal crash of an F-35 occurred on 9 April 2019, when a [Japan Air Self-Defense Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japan_Air_Self-Defense_Force "Japan Air Self-Defense Force") F-35A crashed in the Pacific Ocean east of [Aomori Prefecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aomori_Prefecture "Aomori Prefecture") at an estimated speed of Mach 0.9.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:20-2)[\[390\]](#refnote-:20) The accident report attributed the cause to the pilot's [spatial disorientation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spatial_disorientation "Spatial disorientation"). The pilot's remains and the flight recorder were salvaged, although no data could be retrieved, while the wreckage itself was not salvaged.[&#8202;](#ref-back-spatial_disorientation-4)[\[387\]](#refnote-spatial_disorientation)

On 31 July 2026, a US Marine Corps F-35B crashed and was destroyed at [Marine Corps Air Station Miramar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marine_Corps_Air_Station_Miramar "Marine Corps Air Station Miramar") in San Diego, California. The pilot ejected safely.[&#8202;](#ref-back-605-0)[\[588\]](#refnote-605)[&#8202;](#ref-back-606-0)[\[589\]](#refnote-606)[&#8202;](#ref-back-607-0)[\[590\]](#refnote-607)

### 2026 Iran war

On 19 March 2026, during the [2026 Iran war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Iran_war "2026 Iran war"), a US F-35 flying a combat mission over Iran was forced to make an emergency landing at a regional air base.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:13-0)[\[591\]](#refnote-:13) The [Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Revolutionary_Guard_Corps "Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps") said it had "seriously damaged" the F-35.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:14-0)[\[592\]](#refnote-:14)[&#8202;](#ref-back-:15-0)[\[593\]](#refnote-:15) It was the first time an F-35 sustained damage from enemy fire since it was deployed in combat in 2018.[&#8202;](#ref-back-611-0)[\[594\]](#refnote-611) It was also the first Iranian interception of any US aircraft.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:16-0)[\[595\]](#refnote-:16) The aircraft landed safely.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:13-1)[\[591\]](#refnote-:13)

On 15 September 2026, the [Congressional Budget Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congressional_Budget_Office "Congressional Budget Office") published the report "Estimating the Cost of Combat Operations Against Iran", which listed an F-35A "battle loss" which it estimated could be repaired for $50 million or replaced for the original unit cost of $120 million.[&#8202;](#ref-back-613-0)[\[596\]](#refnote-613)

## UK F-35 exports to Israel and legal challenge

The United Kingdom's participation in the F-35 program became the subject of political and legal controversy during the [Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Gaza_War "2025 Gaza War"). In September 2024, the British government suspended 29 arms export licenses after concluding that there was a clear risk that the items concerned might be used to commit or facilitate serious violations of international humanitarian law in Gaza. Licenses supplying components to the multinational F-35 program were exempted where the government could not identify Israel as the final end user. Ministers argued that suspending all UK supplies to the program would disrupt the global F-35 supply chain and have serious consequences for the defense of the United Kingdom, NATO allies and other international partners.[&#8202;](#ref-back-614-0)[\[597\]](#refnote-614)

The Palestinian human rights organization [Al-Haq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Haq "Al-Haq") challenged the F-35 exemption in judicial review proceedings. In June 2025, the [UK High Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UK_High_Court "UK High Court") dismissed the challenge. The court noted that the structure of the multinational program meant that F-35 aircraft supplied to Israel would contain UK-manufactured components, but held that the central question involved the government's continued participation in a multilateral defense program regarded by ministers as vital to national and international security. It concluded that this "acutely sensitive and political issue" was for the executive, accountable to Parliament and the electorate, rather than the courts.[&#8202;](#ref-back-615-0)[\[598\]](#refnote-615)

Al-Haq sought to appeal the ruling, arguing that the exemption was incompatible with the UK's international law obligations and its own arms-export controls. In November 2025, the [Court of Appeal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Court_of_Appeal_(England_and_Wales) "Court of Appeal (England and Wales)") refused permission for a further appeal, leaving the F-35 exemption in place.[&#8202;](#ref-back-616-0)[\[599\]](#refnote-616)

## Specifications (F-35A)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cc/Lockheed_Martin_F-35A_Lightning_II_3-view_drawing.png/960px-Lockheed_Martin_F-35A_Lightning_II_3-view_drawing.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Lockheed_Martin_F-35A_Lightning_II_3-view_drawing.png) F-35A three-view drawing[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/AGM-158C_flight_testing_begins_on_F-35C.jpg/500px-AGM-158C_flight_testing_begins_on_F-35C.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:AGM-158C_flight_testing_begins_on_F-35C.jpg) F-35C with external hardpoints

_Data from_ Lockheed Martin: F-35 specifications,[&#8202;](#ref-back-LM_F-35A_page-0)[\[600\]](#refnote-LM_F-35A_page)[&#8202;](#ref-back-618-0)[\[601\]](#refnote-618)[&#8202;](#ref-back-lockf35B-0)[\[602\]](#refnote-lockf35B)[&#8202;](#ref-back-lockf35C-0)[\[603\]](#refnote-lockf35C) Lockheed Martin: F-35 weaponry,[&#8202;](#ref-back-weaponry-0)[\[604\]](#refnote-weaponry) Lockheed Martin: F-35 Program Status,[&#8202;](#ref-back-LM_F-35_Status_Fast_Facts-1)[\[125\]](#refnote-LM_F-35_Status_Fast_Facts) F-35 Program brief,[&#8202;](#ref-back-F-35_prog_brief_Sept2006-2)[\[176\]](#refnote-F-35_prog_brief_Sept2006) FY2019 Select Acquisition Report (SAR),[&#8202;](#ref-back-FY2019_President's_Budget_Selected_Acquisition_Report_(SAR-1)[\[406\]](#refnote-FY2019_President's_Budget_Selected_Acquisition_Report_(SAR)_%E2%80%93_F-35_Joint_Strike_Fighter_Aircraft_(F-35)-423) Director of Operational Test & Evaluation[&#8202;](#ref-back-622-0)[\[605\]](#refnote-622)

**General characteristics**

- **Crew:** 1
- **Length:** 51.4ft (15.7m)
- **Wingspan:** 35ft (11m)
- **Height:** 14.4ft (4.4m)
- **Wing area:** 460sqft (43m2)
- **[Aspect ratio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aspect_ratio_(aeronautics) "Aspect ratio (aeronautics)"):** 2.66
- **Empty weight:** 29,300lb (13,290kg)
- **Gross weight:** 49,540lb (22,471kg)
- **Max takeoff weight:** 65,918lb (29,900kg) [&#8202;](#ref-back-623-0)[\[606\]](#refnote-623)
- **Fuel capacity:** 18,250lb (8,278kg) internal
- **Powerplant:** 1 × [Pratt & Whitney F135-PW-100](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pratt_&_Whitney_F135#F135-PW-100 "Pratt & Whitney F135") afterburning [turbofan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turbofan "Turbofan"), 28,000lbf (120kN) thrustdry, 43,000lbf (190kN) with afterburner

**Performance**

- **Maximum speed:** Mach 1.6 at high altitude (1,228mph / 1,976km/h)

  - Mach 1.06, 700 knots (806mph; 1,296km/h) at sea level
- **Range:** 1,500nmi (1,700mi, 2,800km)
- **Combat range:** 669nmi (770mi, 1,239km) interdiction mission (air-to-surface) on internal fuel

  - 760nmi (870mi; 1,410km), air-to-air configuration on internal fuel[&#8202;](#ref-back-Gary_North_Vice_President_Lockheed_Martin_–_Presentation_to_Israel_(2016-0)[\[607\]](#refnote-Gary_North_Vice_President_Lockheed_Martin_–_Presentation_to_Israel_(2016)-624)
- **Service ceiling:** 50,000ft (15,000m)
- **g limits:** +9.0
- **Wing loading:** 107.7lb/sqft (526kg/m2) at gross weight
- **[Thrust/weight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thrust-to-weight_ratio "Thrust-to-weight ratio"):** 0.87 at gross weight (1.07 at loaded weight with 50% internal fuel)

**Armament**

- **Guns:** 1 × [25mm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/25_mm_caliber "25 mm caliber") [GAU-22/A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GAU-12_Equalizer#GAU-22/A "GAU-12 Equalizer") 4-barrel [rotary cannon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotary_cannon "Rotary cannon"), 180 rounds[&#8202;](#ref-back-625-0)[\[N 18\]](#refnote-625)
- **Hardpoints:** 4 × internal stations, 6 × external stations on wings with a capacity of 5,700 pounds (2,600kg) internal, 15,000 pounds (6,800kg) external, 18,000 pounds (8,200kg) total weapons payload, with provisions to carry combinations of:

  - **Missiles:**

    - [Air-to-air missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air-to-air_missile "Air-to-air missile"):

      - [AIM-9X Sidewinder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AIM-9X_Sidewinder "AIM-9X Sidewinder")
      - [AIM-120 AMRAAM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AIM-120_AMRAAM "AIM-120 AMRAAM")
      - [AIM-132 ASRAAM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AIM-132_ASRAAM "AIM-132 ASRAAM")
      - [AIM-260 JATM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AIM-260_JATM "AIM-260 JATM") (being integrated)[&#8202;](#ref-back-626-0)[\[608\]](#refnote-626)
      - [AIM-424 LRAAM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AIM-424_LRAAM "AIM-424 LRAAM") (For F-35C)[&#8202;](#ref-back-627-0)[\[609\]](#refnote-627)
      - [MBDA Meteor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MBDA_Meteor "MBDA Meteor") (Block 4, for F-35B, not before 2027)[&#8202;](#ref-back-628-0)[\[610\]](#refnote-628)[&#8202;](#ref-back-clipped-fin-1)[\[207\]](#refnote-clipped-fin)[&#8202;](#ref-back-integbudget-0)[\[611\]](#refnote-integbudget)
    - [Air-to-surface missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air-to-surface_missile "Air-to-surface missile"):

      - [AGM-88G AARGM-ER](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AGM-88_HARM#AGM-88G_AARGM-ER "AGM-88 HARM") (Block 4)
      - [AGM-158 JASSM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AGM-158_JASSM "AGM-158 JASSM")[&#8202;](#ref-back-JSFrange-1)[\[196\]](#refnote-JSFrange)
      - [SPEAR 3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SPEAR_3 "SPEAR 3") (Block 4, in development, integration contracted)[&#8202;](#ref-back-mbda_spear-1)[\[188\]](#refnote-mbda_spear)[&#8202;](#ref-back-integbudget-1)[\[611\]](#refnote-integbudget)
      - AGM-88J[&#8202;](#ref-back-630-0)[\[612\]](#refnote-630) [Stand-in Attack Weapon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stand-in_Attack_Weapon "Stand-in Attack Weapon") (SiAW)[&#8202;](#ref-back-siawPhase2-0)[\[613\]](#refnote-siawPhase2)
    - [Anti-ship missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-ship_missile "Anti-ship missile"):

      - [AGM-158C LRASM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AGM-158C_LRASM "AGM-158C LRASM")[&#8202;](#ref-back-632-0)[\[614\]](#refnote-632) (being integrated)
      - [Joint Strike Missile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Strike_Missile "Joint Strike Missile") (being integrated)[&#8202;](#ref-back-633-0)[\[615\]](#refnote-633)
      - [Hypersonic Attack Cruise Missile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hypersonic_Attack_Cruise_Missile "Hypersonic Attack Cruise Missile") (Australia; after 2027)
  - **Bombs:**

    - [Joint Direct Attack Munition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Direct_Attack_Munition "Joint Direct Attack Munition")
    - [Paveway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paveway "Paveway")
    - [Precision-guided](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Precision-guided_munition "Precision-guided munition") [glide bomb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glide_bomb "Glide bomb"):

      - [AGM-154 JSOW](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AGM-154_JSOW "AGM-154 JSOW")
      - [GBU-39 Small Diameter Bomb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GBU-39_Small_Diameter_Bomb "GBU-39 Small Diameter Bomb")
      - [GBU-53/B StormBreaker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GBU-53/B_StormBreaker "GBU-53/B StormBreaker")
    - [B61 mod 12](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B61_nuclear_bomb#B61_Mod_12 "B61 nuclear bomb") nuclear bomb[&#8202;](#ref-back-634-0)[\[616\]](#refnote-634)[&#8202;](#ref-back-B61_certified-1)[\[189\]](#refnote-B61_certified)

**Avionics**

- [AN/APG-81](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AN/APG-81 "AN/APG-81") _or_ [AN/APG-85](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AN/APG-85 "AN/APG-85") (Lot 17 onwards) [AESA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Active_Electronically_Scanned_Array "Active Electronically Scanned Array") radar[&#8202;](#ref-back-635-0)[\[617\]](#refnote-635)[&#8202;](#ref-back-636-0)[\[618\]](#refnote-636)
- AN/AAQ-40 Electro-Optical Targeting System[&#8202;](#ref-back-637-0)[\[619\]](#refnote-637)
- [AN/AAQ-37 Electro-Optical Distributed Aperture System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AN/AAQ-37_Electro-Optical_Distributed_Aperture_System "AN/AAQ-37 Electro-Optical Distributed Aperture System")[&#8202;](#ref-back-638-0)[\[620\]](#refnote-638)
- AN/ASQ-239 Barracuda [electronic warfare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_warfare "Electronic warfare")/ [electronic countermeasures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_countermeasures "Electronic countermeasures") system[&#8202;](#ref-back-639-0)[\[621\]](#refnote-639)
- AN/ASQ-242 CNI suite, which includes
  - [Harris Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harris_Corporation "Harris Corporation") [Multifunction Advanced Data Link](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multifunction_Advanced_Data_Link "Multifunction Advanced Data Link") (MADL) communication system
  - [Link 16](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Link_16 "Link 16") data link
  - [SINCGARS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SINCGARS "SINCGARS")
  - An [IFF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Identification_friend_or_foe "Identification friend or foe") interrogator and transponder
  - [HAVE QUICK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HAVE_QUICK "HAVE QUICK")
  - AM, VHF, UHF AM, and UHF FM Radio
  - GUARD survival radio
  - A [radar altimeter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radar_altimeter "Radar altimeter")
  - An [instrument landing system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Instrument_landing_system "Instrument landing system")
  - A [TACAN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TACAN "TACAN") system
  - Instrument carrier landing system
  - A [JPALS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JPALS "JPALS")
  - [TADIL-J](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TADIL-J "TADIL-J") JVMF/VMF

### Differences among variants

|  | F-35A<br>[CTOL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CTOL "CTOL") | F-35B<br>[STOVL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/STOVL "STOVL") | F-35C<br>[CV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carrier-based_aircraft "Carrier-based aircraft") [CATOBAR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CATOBAR "CATOBAR") |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Length | 51.4ft (15.7m) | 51.2ft (15.6m) | 51.5ft (15.7m) |
| Wingspan | 35ft (10.7m) | 35ft (10.7m) | 43ft (13.1m) |
| Height | 14.4ft (4.39m) | 14.3ft (4.36m) | 14.7ft (4.48m) |
| Wing Area | 460sqft (42.74m2) | 460sqft (42.74m2) | 668sqft (62.06m2) |
| Empty weight | 29,300lb (13,300kg) | 32,472lb (14,729kg) | 34,581lb (15,686kg) |
| Internal fuel | 18,250lb (8,278kg) | 13,500lb (6,123kg) | 19,750lb (8,958kg) |
| Weapons payload | 18,000lb (8,160kg) | 15,000lb (6,800kg) | 18,000lb (8,160kg) |
| Max takeoff weight | 70,000lb (31,800kg) class | 60,000lb (27,200kg) class | 70,000lb (31,800kg) class |
| Range | >1,200nmi (2,200km) | >900nmi (1,700km) | >1,200nmi (2,200km) |
| Combat radius on<br>internal fuel | 669nmi (1,239km) | 505nmi (935km) | 670nmi (1,241km) |
| Thrust/weight <br>• full fuel: <br>• 50% fuel: | 0.87<br>1.07 | 0.90<br>1.04 | 0.75<br>0.91 |
| g limit | +9.0 | +7.0 | +7.5 |

## Notes

01. [&#8202;](#refnote-11)[↑](#ref-back-11-0)As of August2023[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Lockheed_Martin_F-35_Lightning_II&action=edit), the program was 80% over budget and 10 years late.[&#8202;](#ref-back-10-0)[\[10\]](#refnote-10)
02. [&#8202;](#refnote-20)[↑](#ref-back-20-0)The F-35A is only certified to carry the B61 Mod 12 variant, with a maximum [yield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_weapon_yield "Nuclear weapon yield") of 50 [kilotons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kilotons "Kilotons").[&#8202;](#ref-back-fasUS2026-2)[\[18\]](#refnote-fasUS2026)
03. [&#8202;](#refnote-27)[↑](#ref-back-27-0)Lockheed acquired General Dynamics fighter division at Fort Worth in 1993 and merged with Martin Marietta in 1995 to form Lockheed Martin.
04. [&#8202;](#refnote-31)[↑](#ref-back-31-0)As these were concept demonstrator aircraft for risk reduction, they did not need to have the internal structure or most subsystems of the final aircraft as a weapon system.
05. [&#8202;](#refnote-33)[↑](#ref-back-33-0)The F-35 swivel nozzle design was pioneered by the Convair Model 200.[&#8202;](#ref-back-32-0)[\[28\]](#refnote-32)
06. [&#8202;](#refnote-37)[↑](#ref-back-37-0)The thrust vectoring nozzle would eventually be replaced by an axisymmetric low-observable nozzle to reduce weight.
07. [&#8202;](#refnote-45)[↑](#ref-back-45-0)FACO is also performed in Italy and Japan for some partner and export customers as part of the industrial benefits from international cooperation.
08. [&#8202;](#refnote-53)[↑](#ref-back-53-0)This first prototype lacked the weight optimization from SWAT.
09. [&#8202;](#refnote-66)[↑](#ref-back-66-0)Early F-35Bs have a service life as low as 2,100hours before retrofits as seen on Lot 9 and later aircraft.
10. [&#8202;](#refnote-91)[↑](#ref-back-91-0)Adaptive cycle engine technology had been under development under [Air Force Life Cycle Management Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_Force_Life_Cycle_Management_Center "Air Force Life Cycle Management Center")'s (AFLCMC) [Adaptive Engine Transition Program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adaptive_Versatile_Engine_Technology "Adaptive Versatile Engine Technology") (AETP) and its precursors.
11. [&#8202;](#refnote-116)[↑](#ref-back-116-0)Turkey was the sole supplier of several F-35 parts, thus forcing the program to find replacement vendors.[&#8202;](#ref-back-115-0)[\[105\]](#refnote-115)
12. [&#8202;](#refnote-123)[↑](#ref-back-123-0)The F-35C has additional ailerons at the folding sections of the wings.
13. [&#8202;](#refnote-149)[↑](#ref-back-149-0)In 2014, Michael Gilmore, Director of Operational Test & Evaluation, stated that "software development, integration in the contractor labs, and delivery of mature capability to flight test continued to be behind schedule."[&#8202;](#ref-back-148-0)[\[136\]](#refnote-148)
14. [&#8202;](#refnote-188)[↑](#ref-back-188-0)Rockwell Collins and Elbit Systems formed the joint venture Vision Systems International (VSI), later renamed Collins Elbit Vision Systems (CEVS).
15. [&#8202;](#refnote-229)[↑](#ref-back-229-0)In 2002, [solid-state laser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solid-state_laser "Solid-state laser") weapons were reportedly being developed for the F-35.[&#8202;](#ref-back-226-0)[\[212\]](#refnote-226)[&#8202;](#ref-back-227-0)[\[213\]](#refnote-227)[&#8202;](#ref-back-228-0)[\[214\]](#refnote-228)
16. [&#8202;](#refnote-284)[↑](#ref-back-284-0)"Bubbling and blistering" of the horizontal tails and tail booms were observed once during flutter tests of the F-35B and C in late 2011; according to the program office, the problem has only occurred once despite numerous attempts to replicate it, and an improved spray-on coating has been implemented since as a mitigation measure. On 17 December 2019, the Pentagon program office closed the issue with no further actions planned, and instead is imposing a time limit on high-speed flight for the F-35B and C to reduce the risk of damaging the stealth coatings and antennas located on the back of the aircraft.[&#8202;](#ref-back-282-0)[\[267\]](#refnote-282)[&#8202;](#ref-back-283-0)[\[268\]](#refnote-283)
17. [&#8202;](#refnote-287)[↑](#ref-back-287-0)Wing drop is an uncommanded roll that can occur during high-g transsonic maneuvering.
18. [&#8202;](#refnote-625)[↑](#ref-back-625-0)F-35B and F-35C have the cannon in an external pod with 220 rounds.
